Marketing StudioLesson / Course Promptvideo460 wordsCinematic / FilmProduct / Commercial

CAMERA: COMPLETELY STATIC throughout the entire video. Fixed position, fixed angle, zero movement. Only subjects and objects move within the frame.

Location part 1: Urban pedestrian overpass bridge. Glass-panel ceiling above letting in diffused grey daylight. Metal railings on both sides, concrete floor, city street with traffic visible far below. Overcast natural lighting.

Location part 2: Dark underground parking garage. Single harsh overhead lamp, tight pool of light on concrete floor, deep shadows, concrete pillars in background.

0–3s: FULL BODY SHOT — static camera, low angle pointing upward. Female model stands centered on the overpass in her own clothes, relaxed stance. She raises the HIGGS sunglasses pinched between two fingers, cocks her wrist back, then THROWS the glasses forward toward the camera with a sharp snap. The sunglasses fly directly at the static lens in full 3D — tumbling and spinning in slow motion, growing larger frame by frame until they nearly fill the screen. Glasses stay fully intact. Neon yellow frame catches daylight. Model stands still in the blurred background. CAMERA DOES NOT MOVE.

4–7s: The glasses reach maximum size, SLOW DOWN, STOP mid-air, then REVERSE on their own — flying back toward the model spinning. As they travel back the model shifts her pose dynamically — she leans her head slightly forward to meet the glasses, lifts her chin, squares her shoulders with attitude. The glasses arc back and land perfectly onto her face. CAMERA STATIC.

8–10s: THE MOMENT the glasses touch her face — LIQUID SCAN TRANSITION: a wave of liquid mercury ripples outward from her face across the entire frame, like a 3D scan pulse sweeping through space. The overpass environment MELTS and DISSOLVES in 3D depth — the bridge, railings, ceiling peel away in layers like liquid geometry dissolving into the scan wave. The dark parking garage MATERIALIZES through the liquid scan, environment rebuilding itself in 3D layers from background to foreground as the wave passes. Simultaneously the full HIGGS outfit liquifies onto her body: white jersey t-shirt, neon yellow puffer jacket, black track pants with neon green stripe, grey sneakers with neon yellow laces — all five items flow onto her in one fluid wave synced with the environmental scan. The model strikes a new powerful pose as the outfit locks in — weight shifted to one leg, one hand at side, chin up. CAMERA STATIC.

11–15s: MEDIUM CLOSE-UP — static camera at chest-to-head height. She stands fully dressed in all HIGGS items in the parking garage. Hard overhead lamp light, sharp shadows. She adjusts sunglasses with one finger, holds the pose, stares directly into camera with cold confident expression. No glow, no outlines on body. Cinematic grade — deep blacks, punchy contrast, neon yellow only as real material color on clothing. 9:16 vertical.

# HIGGS — 5 CLIPS

LOCATION: Indoor room. Large white-framed floor-to-ceiling glass door centered in background. Dark night outside. Grey linen curtains floor-length on both sides. Warm hardwood parquet floor. Cool blue backlight from behind the door, soft frontal key light on face. Cold blue-teal cinematic color grade.
CAMERA: STATIC, FULL BODY SHOT, eye level. Same angle all 5 clips, never moves.
IMPORTANT: Female model has NO bag, NO backpack, NO purse on her at any point in any clip. Hands are empty unless specified.

---

## CLIP 1 — 4s
Female model stands centered in her own clothes. No bag, no backpack. She reaches off-frame, grabs the white HIGGS jersey t-shirt, pulls it over her head and on — adjusts it, smooths it down. Looks into camera. No effects. 9:16 vertical.

---

## CLIP 2 — 4s — JACKET
Female model stands centered, already wearing the white HIGGS t-shirt. No bag, no backpack. She reaches off-frame, grabs the HIGGS puffer jacket, shrugs it on — one arm then the other — adjusts collar with both hands. Looks into camera. No effects. 9:16 vertical.

---

## CLIP 3 — 4s — PANTS + SNEAKERS

Female model stands centered, already wearing HIGGS t-shirt and jacket. No bag, no backpack. Already wearing HIGGS track pants with neon green side stripe and grey HIGGS sneakers — changed off-screen. She runs one hand down the side stripe, lifts one foot and stomps on the parquet once. Looks into camera. No effects. 9:16 vertical.

---

## CLIP 4 — 3s — SUNGLASSES
Female model stands centered, fully dressed in HIGGS outfit. No bag, no backpack. She holds the HIGGS sunglasses between two fingers, slides them onto her face in one smooth motion. Looks into camera — cold confident expression. No effects. 9:16 vertical.

---

## CLIP 5 — 3s — FINAL
Female model from stands centered, complete HIGGS outfit, sunglasses on. No bag, no backpack. Arms relaxed at sides. She squares her shoulders, looks directly into camera. Says: "That's it." Holds the pose cold and still. 9:16 vertical.

SCENE CONTEXT

The camera is restless and handheld through the fight, rigid body-locked in the fall, and finishes inside his eyes. Both cats act with small, expressive anime emotions — determination, fury, effort, surprise — always readable, never mugging. Anime style, ferocious pace, small grounded visual effects on every impact, no dialogue.

<<<image_1>>> is the HERO, the SWAT CAT — an anthropomorphic grey tabby cat with dark stripes, sharp yellow eyes and white muzzle, in a navy tactical uniform with rolled sleeves, a black plate carrier with radio, black gloves, knee pads, boots, and a black helmet mounting a four-lens night-vision device flipped UP; striped tail. Identical to this reference throughout. His acting: focused professional determination — narrowed eyes, ears forward and controlled — with small honest beats breaking through: a wince of effort on the hardest block, a grim set of the jaw as he finishes the fight, calm resolve smoothing his face in the freefall.  
<<<image_2>>> is the VILLAIN, the ROGUE CAT — an anthropomorphic Siamese cat with a dark mask-like face, ice-blue eyes and a scar across the muzzle, in torn black-and-grey tactical gear: a ragged sleeveless shirt, patched plate carrier, battered helmet with a four-lens night-vision device where one lens is cracked and dead, fingerless gloves, boots; brown tail. Identical to this reference throughout. His acting: snarling feral aggression — bared teeth, ears pinned flat, pupils slitted — cracking into one wide-eyed flash of surprise when his knife arm is caught, and ending dazed and glaring weakly from under the spilled cargo.  
<<<image_3>>> is the LOCATION — the cargo hold of a large aircraft: a long ribbed vaulted ceiling with strip lights, cream paneled walls with tie-down hatches, rows of floor rails studded with yellow-and-red cargo clamps, a ladder against the right wall, and the hold opening at its far end into darkness — the open rear cargo ramp, night sky beyond. Identical to this reference in geometry and light — and dressed with cargo: stacks of olive wooden crates and steel cases strapped along both walls, a netted pallet of duffel bags and boxes mid-hold, a small strapped-down quad bike near the left wall, a cluster of steel fuel drums by the ladder, loose straps and a hanging cargo hook swaying with the engine drone.

FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING  
First frame: mid-fight, mid-swing — the SWAT cat and the rogue cat already locked in close combat at the center of the cargo hold, the rogue's teeth bared and the SWAT cat's eyes narrowed in focus, crate stacks and the netted pallet around them, the strapped quad bike at the left wall, fuel drums by the ladder, the open dark ramp yawning at the far end behind them, strip lights streaking overhead, the handheld camera already lunging with the action. No calm before it; the video opens inside the violence.

OPTICS  
Anime cinematic rendering, crisp character line work over painted backgrounds; a wide 65° feel in the hold, tightening to an intimate 50° body-locked framing in the snorricam. No zooms — every framing change comes from camera movement or the rig.

CAMERA — THREE JOINED MOVEMENTS, ONE UNBROKEN TAKE  
[0-4.5s] THE FIGHT — restless handheld: the camera never settles for a single beat — lunging with strikes, whipping with reversals, ducking under a kick, shoving in close on impacts and tearing back out, weaving between crate stacks as the fighters crash through them, always breathing, always shaking with the hits, strip lights smearing overhead, the open ramp swinging in and out of frame. It is never stable, never on a tripod, never still.  
[4.5-5s] THE DIVE TRANSITION: as the SWAT cat sprints for the ramp and launches himself backward off its edge into the dark, the camera dives after him and LOCKS ON — becoming a rigid snorricam mounted to his chest harness.  
[5-8.5s] THE SNORRICAM — rigid body-mounted freefall on his back: exactly like a real snorricam rig — the camera fixed on a rigid arm extending from his chest, aimed down at his face and upper body from a constant close distance, so HE is welded to the frame, perfectly steady, while the entire world wheels behind and around him: he falls back-first, facing the sky — the plane's silhouette and its open ramp shrinking above him, moonlit clouds rotating, and tumbling cargo falling with him through the frame — crates spinning past shedding straps, a duffel bag whipping by, loose boxes scattering their contents into the slipstream, wind tearing at his fur. He is rock-solid in frame; everything else falls and turns.  
[8.5-10s] THE ORBIT INTO THE GOGGLES: still falling on his back, he reaches up and snaps the four-lens night-vision device DOWN over his eyes and rolls his body over to face the earth — the camera releasing from the rig into one small smooth orbit around his helmet, arcing from his profile to head-on, and then flying forward INTO the glowing green glass of a goggle lens — the lens filling the frame until the image becomes his POV: the anime night city sprawling far below in green night-vision — phosphor-green monochrome, glowing window grids, streets like bright veins, soft bloom on every light, a hint of scanline and vignette at the edges, the horizon tilting gently with his fall — and cutting across the green view BELOW him, another airplane sweeps past: a large aircraft crossing left to right beneath his fall, its engines blooming as hot green flares in the night-vision, its navigation lights pulsing bright, a faint green contrail shearing behind it as it thunders across the city glow and carries on. The video ends inside this green POV, the second plane still crossing. One continuous take: no cuts, no teleports; parallax and motion alive in every frame.

ACTION TIMING  
0-1.5s — THE EXCHANGE: the rogue cat slashes with a combat knife in fast anime combos, snarling with bared teeth, ears pinned flat — the blade scrapes the SWAT cat's forearm guard in a thin trail of orange sparks, the SWAT cat's face tightening in a small wince of effort before his eyes narrow harder; he weaves and answers with two sharp jabs that knock the rogue back INTO a crate stack — the top crate bursting apart in a spray of splinters and spilling packed gear across the floor rails, a small dust burst blooming on the impact.  
1.5-3s — THE TURN: the rogue lunges too hard, fury wide in his ice-blue eyes — the SWAT cat catches the knife arm, and for one held drawing the rogue's snarl cracks into wide-eyed surprise — the SWAT cat twists the arm into a lock and slams him across the strapped quad bike, the machine rocking hard against its straps, a mirror snapping off and skittering away; the knife flies loose and clangs off a fuel drum with a bright spark ping; a knee to the gut folds the rogue, a spinning elbow cracks across his helmet — the dead lens shattering in a tiny burst of glass glitter.  
3-4.5s — THE FINISH: one clean anime-fast roundhouse sends the rogue cat crashing through the netted pallet — bags and boxes avalanching down over him in a whump of dust, leaving him tangled and limp under the spilled cargo, glaring weakly through half-lidded dazed eyes, beaten; the SWAT cat holds one breath in a strong stepped pose, ears flat, jaw set in a grim beat of resolve — loose papers and packing straw still fluttering down through the air around him — then his ears swivel toward the ramp, and he turns and SPRINTS for it, vaulting a fallen crate.  
4.5-5s — THE JUMP: three pounding strides, he plants both boots on the ramp's edge and kicks off BACKWARD into the night, arms open, eyes steady — his wake dragging two loose crates and a duffel bag tumbling off the ramp after him.  
5-8.5s — THE FALL: rigid snorricam on his back — his face settling from fight-tension into calm resolve against the roaring wind, a slow blink, whiskers streaming, fur rippling, the sky and shrinking plane wheeling behind him, crates and spilled gear tumbling through frame around him at their own rates; he checks the parachute strap on his shoulder with one paw — a small pat, packed and ready on his back — the faintest satisfied twitch at the corner of his muzzle, and lets the fall run.

Final frame: the green city and the crossing plane through his eyes.

Sun camera-right, dimming from 40% to blackout: while any direct light survives it strikes the man's back-right shoulder, and his shadow — soft and fading — falls ahead-left of him along his run line.
FIRST FRAME / BLOCKING
47° frontal MS: the man laboring straight at camera through deep sand, hood framing his gritted face, the three riders small but distinct in the background behind him, the storm wall filling the sky behind them.
FORMAT MODE
Timed multishot. Cuts only at the specified points, the camera does not cut on its own.
0.0s to 2.0s — LENS LOCK: 47° frontal tracking, retreating just ahead of the man at his labored 13 km/h, MS from the front. He fights straight at camera — short driving strides, each footfall sinking to the ankle and sliding back, knees pumping high, breath tearing — the weak sun catching his back-right shoulder, his faint shadow thrown ahead of him toward the lens; behind him the three-bird pack GROWS in frame shot-long, legs a rapid scissoring blur, gliding over the sand that swallows his feet; the LEAD rises in his stirrups with a long ululating cry, the SECOND answers with sharp whistles, the THIRD barks a guttural command — the pack surges on the cries; the storm's shadow races across the sand and swallows the ground behind them. Shot holds 47° to the cut.
2.0s HARD CUT
2.0s to 4.0s — LENS LOCK: 63° from BEHIND the pack, tracking at their 50 km/h, low over the riders' backs — hunting geometry: the three black-robed backs fill the near frame, robes cracking, green tail plumes streaming beneath them; between their bobbing shoulders, ahead and below, the small laboring figure of the man running camera-LEFT away from the lens — and beyond him the oasis is REAL now: palm trunks, thrashing fronds, brush, meters of ground closing; the LEAD throws his head back and howls a war cry, the others answering in overlapping shouts, heels drumming the birds' flanks; daylight dying at the frame edges as the storm's murk bleeds in from behind. Shot holds 63° to the cut.
4.0s HARD CUT
4.0s to 5.5s — LENS LOCK: 47° low tracking beside the man at his pace, tight MS in profile, facing camera-left. He crashes through the first brush at the oasis edge, the last amber light grazing his back-right shoulder — and his eyes LOCK camera-left onto the case under the nearest palm, the green button glowing in the palm's shade eight meters ahead; his whole body redirects toward it, one arm already reaching, the riders' cries and the wind roar stacking behind him — and the frame itself darkens beat by beat as the storm wall's leading edge eats the sky behind him. Shot holds 47° to the cut.
5.5s HARD CUT
5.5s to 7.0s — LENS LOCK: 29° low angle at sand level beside the case, locked off. Internal timing of this single locked shot: at 5.5s the case sits large in the left foreground, green button glowing, and the storm wall is ALREADY IN FRAME — a churning ochre cliff filling the entire right third, racing camera-left at 80 km/h, sand streaking horizontally ahead of it. At 5.9s the man DIVES into frame from the right ahead of the wall — a full-body lunge through the air, hood pinned by the wind, arm at maximum stretch toward the button, still meters of open air between his fingertips and the case. At 6.2s, mid-flight, the wall CATCHES him — the churning murk swallows the man first, whole, while he is still airborne and still meters short, his outstretched arm the last of him to vanish. At 6.4s the murk rolls on across the remaining gap and drowns the case — the green glow the last thing visible, untouched, then gone. From 6.5s to 7.0s the frame is solid churning ochre, sand grains streaking horizontally past the lens. The wall moves faster than the man flies; it closes the distance before he can. Shot holds 29° to the end.
CAMERA
Shot 1 frontal retreat at the man's pace, 1 cm rhythmic tremor from the closing strides. Shot 2 low tracking behind the pack, sharing its jolting rhythm, unstabilized. Shot 3 low lateral tracking beside the man, tight. Shot 4 locked off at sand level as the storm crosses and overruns the frame. No drift mid-segment.
PERFORMANCE
The man: desperate labor inside the hood — mouth open, breath ragged, effort in every muscle; the moment he sees the case, his face sharpens from panic to purpose; the dive is total commitment, eyes fixed on the button — then the wall takes him mid-reach, arm still stretched into empty air. The riders: predatory momentum — rising in stirrups, heads thrown back on the cries, heels working the flanks, bodies pitching with the stride. The ostriches: heads low and thrust ahead, surging on every shout.
PHYSICS
His run obeys sand physics: footfalls sink to the ankle and slide back, sand spraying from his kicks, a churned trench behind him; the brush at the oasis edge whips and snaps as he crashes through. The dive is true ballistics — launched off one driving leg, body stretched horizontal, sand bursting where he took off; a human dive covers two meters of air, and the case lies eight meters out — his trajectory falls short by physics alone. The storm wall moves at 80 km/h, four times faster than his flight — it overtakes him mid-air from behind. The ostriches' legs cycle in a rapid blur, ground contact a fraction of a beat, wide two-toed feet barely sinking. Palm fronds thrash and bend leeward. Airborne sand thickens in visible layers; grains streak horizontally as the wall arrives; the wind plasters the hood tighter against his head.
LIGHTING
Sun camera-right, 40% dimmed at frame one and dying fast — while it lasts it strikes the man's back-right shoulder, his fading shadow falling ahead-left along his run line; the storm's shadow sweeps the sand in shot 1, murk bleeds into the frame edges in shot 2, deep amber gloom by shot 3. Shot 4 is exposed for the button's green glow, the brightest element in frame — a saturated glow in the dimness, the diving man reading as a dark shape with a faint amber rim, detail sacrificed to the gloom — the man vanishes into the murk first, then the green glow drowns last, still untouched, then near-total ochre blackout.
AUDIO
Rising deafening wind roar underneath everything; the rapid drumming of six ostrich feet closing; his tearing breath and the crunch-slide of sinking footfalls; the riders' voices cutting through the wind — ululating cry, sharp whistles, guttural overlapping shouts, one final war cry — each closer than the last; the crash of brush; then the wall hits with a concussive WHUMP of sand that cuts the riders' cries dead, and from inside the murk a muffled scream — "NOOO!!!" — swallowed by the roar.
STYLE
Photoreal live-action adventure film look, anamorphic widescreen, fine grain, 8K. SCENE CONTEXT
Bright day at sea aboard a sailing galleon. Captain Eduardo bursts out of the sterncastle door onto the deck; his scarlet macaw lands on his LEFT shoulder mid-stride. He runs up to the quarterdeck where a lookout crewman watches the horizon through a brass spyglass, takes the spyglass and looks himself: a distant island, and a violent optical crash zoom finds a small futuristic hard case on the beach. Then a second crewman runs up, grabs his arm and points the OTHER way, astern — Eduardo turns: a black-sailed pirate ship very far behind them, a speck on the horizon. He does not raise the spyglass — he just stands and stares at the distant black sails, holding the look.
ACTIVE REFERENCES
@eduardo — lean pirate captain, dark curly hair falling free from under a dusty mustard-yellow cloth bandana, a small white shark tooth pinned to the front of the bandana above his temple, thin moustache, gold hoop earrings, cream linen shirt under a worn brown leather waistcoat, cloth sash and leather belts. 100% matches the reference; studio sheet layout NOT inherited.
@parrot_sheet — scarlet macaw, red body, blue-and-yellow wing feathers, small leather shoulder harness. 100% matches the reference; it flies in and rides Eduardo's left shoulder.
@eduardo_crew — weathered pirate crewmen from the reference group (bandana, rough shirt, vest). 100% match the reference; TWO of them appear: the lookout at the quarterdeck rail, and a second runner who arrives in CUT 4 pointing astern.
@spyglass_sheet — collapsible brass spyglass with dark leather-wrapped barrel sections. 100% matches the reference; starts in the lookout's hands, ends at Eduardo's eye.
@main_ship_sheet — Eduardo's galleon: pale square sails, tall wooden sterncastle, raised quarterdeck. 100% matches the reference; controls hull, deck, masts and rigging only.
@ocean_location — calm bright sea, glittering sun path, hazy horizon. Controls water and sky atmosphere only.
@island_location — small lone island: dense dark-green jungle cover, a curved white-sand beach on one side, grey rocky cliff edges, turquoise shallows ringing the shore. 100% matches the reference; it is the island seen on the horizon and inside the spyglass view.
@button — small futuristic hard case: matte-black armored corners, neon acid-green side panels, brushed-steel top plate with a glowing green star-shaped button. 100% matches the reference; it appears ONLY inside the zoomed spyglass view of CUT 3, lying on the beach.
@villain_ship_sheet — enemy pirate galleon: black sails, acid-green skull-and-crossed-swords on the mainsail, dark carved hull. 100% matches the reference; revealed VERY far astern in CUT 4 as a tiny silhouette on the horizon — never seen closer in this beat.
LOCATION MAP

FORMAT MODE
Sequence of cuts, no timecodes — cuts only at the specified points, the camera does not cut on its own.
CUT 1 — 63° handheld follow: the door bursts open, Eduardo comes out in a strange hurried scurry — up on TIPTOE, quick tiny mincing steps, both arms half-raised in front of him with elbows out, hands hovering at chest height, shoulders slightly hunched — comically odd, but FAST, covering the deck at 10 km/h. @parrot_sheet sweeps in from off-frame upper-right, wings braking, and lands on his left shoulder without breaking the scurry. Door kicks open from inside. PERFORMANCE
Urgency without panic: breath fast through the nose, eyes fixed forward during the run. At the eyepiece his face stills completely — squint tightens, lips part a fraction when the case appears. Pore-level skin realism, sun catch-lights, spray-damp sheen on the temples.

SCENE CONTEXT  
One continuous 10-second infinity zoom-out shot for a bright, hyper-glossy Y2K music video, in which the entire visible world spins slowly and continuously clockwise inside the frame from the very first frame to the very last, completing exactly one full revolution. The camera never cuts. It pulls backward the whole time while the world turns: horizons tilt, walls rotate past, floors climb the frame edges. Each scene is revealed to be a live image nested inside an object in the next scene. Every nested hand-off is purely geometric — the ongoing scene stays fully solid and opaque while its container simply grows in around it as the camera pulls back; nothing ever fades, dissolves, or turns transparent. The final scene is the same city street as the opening, arriving right-side-up again as the revolution completes, closing a perfect loop.

ACTIVE REFERENCES  
<<<image_1>>> is the STREET MAN character reference — Y2K man: yellow striped beanie, yellow open-mesh tee over white long-sleeve, chunky silver chains with a square pendant, white bead bracelet, black jorts with a huge silver ball chain, yellow-black-white sneakers. Identical to this reference wherever he appears.  
<<<image_2>>> is the BATH GIRL character reference — Y2K girl: long black hair with wispy bangs, hot-pink halter crop top with a white satin bow, silver star belly chain, star necklace, pink bead bracelet. Identical to this reference (in the tub, visible waist-up above the foam).  
<<<image_3>>> is the ARCADE BOY character reference — Y2K boy: backwards royal-blue cap over dark curls, thin mustache, blue mesh V-neck jersey over white tee, shell necklace, stud earrings, baggy washed denim, blue-white sneakers. Identical to this reference.  
<<<image_4>>> is the BARBER GIRL character reference — Y2K girl: long braids with lime-green strands, lime-green off-shoulder long-sleeve crop top, gold bangles and earrings, gold plate necklace, lime belt with a jeweled buckle and a lime bandana at her hip, grey baggy pants, lime fuzzy leg warmers, green-and-white nails. Identical to this reference.  
<<<image_5>>> is the STREET location reference — sunlit London-style street: red, yellow and teal shopfronts, brick uppers, blue sky, a red barbershop storefront with a chrome barber pole at frame left. Controls scene 1 and scene 5. All existing signage stays exactly as in the reference; no new readable text ever appears anywhere in the video.

TEXT-DEFINED LOCATIONS (no image references — build these from description, in the same bright Y2K world)  
BATHROOM (scene 2): a vivid hot-pink glossy-tiled bathroom with turquoise trim, a white bathtub crowned with dense turquoise foam, gold vintage fixtures, colorful bottles on a pink shelf, and a gold-framed picture hanging on the tiled wall directly above the tub — the frame where the live street scene lives.  
ARCADE (scene 3): a dark neon arcade with rows of decorated cabinets, curved glowing CRT screens, red joysticks and colorful buttons, pink and green neon tubes glowing against the dark — the foreground cabinet's CRT is where the live bathroom scene plays.  
BARBERSHOP (scene 4): a bright modern barbershop with a black-and-white checkerboard floor, red leather barber chairs, bulb-lit mirrors, shelves of colorful product bottles, a chrome-and-glass front looking onto the street, a spinning barber pole visible outside, and a slim wall-mounted flatscreen TV high on the wall — the TV where the live arcade scene plays.

In the very first second the whole street already begins to tilt clockwise in frame — the horizon visibly rotating, buildings starting to lean. This exact live image is what later fills the gold frame in the bathroom.

FORMAT MODE  
16:9 horizontal, single continuous take, 10 seconds.

OPTICS  
84° diagonal field of view, classic wide rectilinear character. Lens locked for the whole shot; all zoom-out is physical camera pull-back, no focal-length drift, no fisheye.

CAMERA  
The camera does two things at once for all 10 seconds without ever stopping: it pulls backward continuously, and it rolls clockwise on its optical axis in one slow unbroken spin, so the world inside the frame is always visibly turning — never level, never holding, never counter-rotating. The rotation is slow enough that faces stay readable but always clearly in motion. Orientation checkpoints: at 2.5s the world lies fully sideways (90°), at 5s fully upside-down (180°), at 7.5s sideways the other way (270°), and only in the final frame at 10s does the world return exactly right-side-up. The spin carries seamlessly across every scene transition with no reset and no pause.

ACTION TIMING  
0.0–1.5s — STREET: the man from <<<image_1>>> bounces on the beat with one big clear arm swing while the street from <<<image_5>>> tilts clockwise past 30-40°; the camera pulls straight back and, at full solidity, gold frame edges grow in from outside the frame around the still-live street image — the street stays fully opaque and vivid inside the frame, never fading.

8.6–10.0s — STREET AGAIN: the camera keeps pulling back along the same sunlit street, the barbershop storefront at frame left, the man from <<<image_1>>> visible again center frame in the distance mid-bounce; the street rotates through the final degrees and settles exactly right-side-up on the last frame, recreating the opening composition.

PHYSICS  
Gravity stays true to each world even as the frame rotates: foam clings and slides downward on the shoulders of the girl from <<<image_2>>>, the cape drapes naturally on the girl from <<<image_4>>>, the barber pole spins on its own axis — only the camera's orientation turns, never the physics. Every nested image is a fully opaque physical surface at 100% visibility from the moment it appears to the moment it leaves frame: the framed print is solid paper with slight glare, the CRT is solid glowing curved glass with scanlines, the TV is a solid flat panel with faint glass glare — none are ever translucent, none ever show anything through themselves, and no two scenes are ever visible blended in the same pixels. Each nested image stays anchored inside its frame or bezel as it shrinks — it does not slide, peel, detach, fade, or dissolve. The storefront glass carries a brief real reflection of the street as the camera passes through the window plane. Each nested surface keeps correct perspective as the camera recedes and rotates.

LIGHTING  
Street: clean high midday sun, crisp shadows, saturated color. Bathroom: bright bounced light off pink tiles, glossy highlights on foam and skin, gold fixtures glinting. Arcade: saturated neon pink and green practicals, CRT glow spilling onto the jersey of the boy from <<<image_3>>>, face readable. Barbershop: warm bulb-light from the mirror frames, clean bright key, color pops from bottle shelves and red chairs. The barbershop-to-street transition rides a natural interior-to-daylight exposure shift.

AUDIO  
No music, no dialogue, no voices — silent visual designed for a track to be laid over in the edit.

STYLE  
Bright, hyper-glossy Y2K music-video aesthetic: bubblegum pinks, lime greens, chrome and silver jewelry, mesh jerseys, baby tees, bead accessories, baggy denim, glossy plastic sheen, punchy saturated digital cinematography with a subtle early-2000s glamour bloom — playful, flirty, maximalist.

QUALITY  
Faces, gestures and nested objects stay sharp and readable throughout the continuous spin, with natural subtle motion blur from the rotation and pull-back. Every nested image remains at full opacity and full saturation through its entire transition — no cross-dissolves, no double exposures, no ghost overlaps between scenes, no translucent frames or screens. The CRT reveal stays crisp: bezel edges clean, screen image unbroken and solid. No ghosting, no flicker beyond the intentional CRT scanline quality, no duplicated limbs, no frame-skipping stutter, no cuts, and the rotation never freezes, never snaps level, and never reverses.

CAMERA — CONSTANT FLIGHT

3.5-5.5s: the car hits the bottom and slings through its hard banked turn, the five friends leaning in unison, the front woman throwing a fist up; the still-flying camera catches them in profile as it continues its lap, the carousel and striped stalls streaking behind them.  
5.5-8s: the car swoops through the far bend and rises onto the next straight as the camera's lap curves around to meet it nearly head-on — the camera still traveling laterally as the car races toward it — the riders' breathless laughing faces growing larger, hair settling, the dark-haired woman's arms up now too. Final frame: the car racing toward the moving camera, five beaming faces readable, the Ferris wheel still sliding across the sky behind them, sun flaring at the frame edge.

PHYSICS  
Real coaster physics on every body: weightless float at the crest, violent upward hair and clothing whip through the drop, unified sideways lean through the banks, natural bounce on the rise; hands grip the safety bar except when raised. The camera flies with real aerial momentum — one continuous banked circular flight with gentle radius breathing, spatially consistent with the fairground's geometry, never clipping through the coaster structure, riders or scenery, and never coming to rest. Background parallax and motion blur match the true relative speed between the flying camera and the racing car at every moment; there is no frame in the video where the background is stationary. Constant real-time speed throughout, no slow motion anywhere.

LIGHTING  
Bright golden afternoon sun in a deep blue sky with scattered white clouds: the sun travels around the frame as the camera flies its lap — flaring into the lens on one side of the circle, backlighting flying hair on the opposite side — faces lit by open-sky bounce, the fairground saturated and sunlit in every direction. Natural lens flares from the real sun only.

AUDIO  
Roaring wind rush that never dies down — the constant airflow of a camera in flight; the rhythmic clatter of coaster wheels swelling and softening as the flying camera nears and recedes from the track; five voices screaming, whooping and laughing in real bursts; the fairground rotating through the soundfield — carousel organ, crowd murmur, a ride bell — panning continuously with the camera's lap; a shared burst of breathless laughter as the car races toward the lens at the end. No dialogue lines, no narration, no music track.

STYLE  
Hyper-realistic FPV-drone cinematography with premium commercial polish: crisp sunlit image, saturated fairground palette of reds, golds and sky blue, one masterful continuous flight line graded like a feel-good summer ad, every frame bursting with motion and joy.

QUALITY  
Stable rendering at constant real speed: all five riders stay consistent in identity, seating order and clothing for the whole shot, faces readable whenever they face the camera; no ghosting, no duplicated limbs, no warping of the track, car or Ferris wheel as the camera flies around them; motion blur natural to the relative speeds, resolving sharp in the final head-on frames.

POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS

Let’s see what we got.

That's how you add something into your shot — plus a camera move you never filmed.
Now let’s move to the one you've been waiting for, and the hardest thing we'll do all video: the handheld cinematic showcase. The kind of big-budget CGI shots that normally need a whole studio.

This is the hardest one so far. On a locked-off or steady shots we have already seen, the AI has it easy — the frame barely changes, so it just paints the effect in. But the second the camera's in your hand, everything's moving chaotically — the angle, the parallax, the shake — and the effect has to track all of it without falling apart. That's this stage: fully handheld, me and the camera moving the whole time. And here's the whole trick: I shoot each clip already knowing what I want it to see in it, then one simple prompt rebuilds the entire world around me. Same clip in, a different planet out. Let’s see the first one
**Wing-walker on a biplane**

I have this one prompt

prompt 1 wing walker

Lesson / Course Promptimage162 wordsCinematic / Film

A suspended, almost meditative wide shot looking straight up at a twilight sky: a single blade — long, dark, and angular — falls point-first toward the camera in slow motion, bisecting the frame vertically. A blazing white sun sits low on the left horizon, casting lens flare across the arena. The dark silhouettes of monumental warrior statues ring the arena rim against the fading sky, small wispy clouds drifting above. This cuts to a decisive ground-level shot of the massive armored gladiator crashing backward onto the red arena sand, his horned helmet and heavy pauldrons slamming into the earth, limbs splayed, one gauntleted hand clawing at the air in a final spasm. Dust explodes outward from the impact. The arena grandstands loom above, indistinct in the hazy golden light. The atmosphere is one of sudden, violent finality — the decisive moment of a brutal contest — rendered in a slow-motion, high-contrast cinematic style evoking the iconic death sequences of classical gladiatorial epics.

Objects — furniture, papers, cups — fly outward in a telekinetic shockwave.
